Модуль 6 · Колоночные базы и хранилища данных

27 вопросов · Лекции 9 и 9-bis и часть занятия E05 про Cassandra

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

True or false: Cassandra stores the values of each column of a column family together, separately from the other columns, as a column-oriented database such as Vertica does.

  • True Это колоночное хранение со слайдов (pp. 401–407). Cassandra держит столбцы строки вместе под её ключом строки: "think of cassandra as row-oriented" (p. 417).
  • False Cassandra — wide-column store: строка, которую находят по ключу, хранит все свои столбцы вместе (p. 417). Хранить каждый столбец отдельно — это аналитическое колоночное хранилище со слайдов (pp. 401–407).
Показать образец ответа

False. Cassandra is a wide-column (column-family) store: each row is uniquely identified by its key and keeps all its columns, sorted by name, together, so it should be thought of as row-oriented. Storing each column separately is the design of column-oriented databases such as Vertica or C-Store, built for OLAP: a query reads only the columns it needs, while inserts and whole-record reads touch every column. Cassandra is called columnar because of its column families and its very wide rows, and it is built for write-heavy workloads.

Ловушка — в слове «column», общем для двух разных конструкций (разделы 2 и 3). За неправильные ответы может быть штраф, о нём объявляют прямо перед экзаменом: отвечай только то, что можешь обосновать.

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Explain how a column-oriented database stores a table and compare it with a row-oriented database: advantages, disadvantages, and the workloads each one suits.

Показать образец ответа

A row-oriented database stores each record contiguously; a column-oriented database stores each column contiguously, in its own storage. A query that needs k of the m columns therefore reads only those k columns in a column store, while a row store reads whole records and may read unnecessary data. The column layout also compresses better (the values of a column share a type and repeat, so techniques such as run-length encoding work well) and improves bandwidth utilisation, code pipelining and cache locality. Its disadvantages are increased disk seek time (a record is spread over many files), an increased cost of inserts (one write per column) and increased tuple reconstruction costs (records must be rebuilt by position). Column stores therefore suit read-mostly, read-intensive, large repositories — OLAP and data mining — while row stores suit OLTP, with frequent inserts and updates of whole records.

У полного ответа четыре части: две раскладки, довод про чтение (k из m столбцов), плюсы и минусы словами слайдов (pp. 403–404) и типы нагрузки. Сильное дополнение — график со слайда (p. 405): колоночное хранилище проигрывает, только когда запрос выбирает почти весь кортеж.

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

The Quarter column of a sales table, sorted by quarter, is stored with run-length encoding as (Q1, 1, 300), (Q2, 301, 350), (Q3, 651, 500), (Q4, 1151, 600). Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.

  • (Q2, 301, 350) means that Q2 is stored at the positions from 301 to 350. Третье число — длина, а не конец: Q2 занимает 350 позиций с 301 по 650.
  • The table has 1,750 rows. Последняя серия заканчивается на 1151 + 600 − 1 = 1750 — столько же, сколько 300 + 350 + 500 + 600.
  • Position 700 holds Q3. Q3 начинается с 651 и покрывает 500 позиций, до 1150.
  • The Price column of the same table would shrink just as much with the same encoding. Соседние цены редко повторяются: каждая цена превратилась бы в тройку, втрое больше сырого размера, — поэтому слайд (p. 407) оставляет Price несжатым.
  • Sorted by customer instead, the same Quarter column would usually compress far less. Сериям нужны равные значения рядом; другой порядок сортировки их разбивает.
Показать образец ответа

The second, third and fifth statements. Each triple is (value, start_pos, run_length), so (Q2, 301, 350) covers the positions 301 to 650; the last run ends at 1151 + 600 − 1 = 1750; position 700 lies in the run of Q3, from 651 to 1150. Run-length encoding pays only when equal values are adjacent: the Quarter column is sorted and the prices are not, so the encoding is chosen column by column and Price stays raw.

Числа взяты со слайда (p. 407), так что ответ можно сверить со слайдом. Классическая ошибка — принять run_length за конечную позицию. За неверные отметки может быть штраф: отмечай только то, что можешь обосновать.

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Describe the data model of Cassandra as presented in the lecture — keyspace, column family, row, column and super column. Explain why a column family is called a sparse table and what the timestamp of a column is used for.

Показать образец ответа

A keyspace is the outermost container, roughly a database and typically one per application; some settings, such as the replication, are configured per keyspace. It contains column families, the analogue of tables, which group records of a similar but not identical kind; their settings include the comparator and the type, standard or super. A column family holds rows, each uniquely identified by its row key; Cassandra should be thought of as row-oriented, because a row's columns are stored and returned together. A column has three parts: a name (bytes; it determines the sort order, is used in queries and is indexed), a value (bytes; values are not queried) and a timestamp. A super column groups columns under a common name: it has a name and a map of columns instead of a value, and its sub-column names are not indexed. A column family is a sparse table because each row stores only the columns it has: a missing column costs nothing and is not a NULL. The timestamp (clock) is supplied with every write and resolves conflicts between replicas: the last write wins.

Дай цепочку вложенности по порядку и три части столбца с их свойствами (p. 420). «Sparse» и «last write wins» — два «почему», о которых спрашивает вопрос. Если цитируешь пример настройки keyspace со слайда, partitioner, добавь, что на самом деле он задаётся для всего кластера (красная ручка к слайду (p. 413)).

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Explain the difference between the domain-based model of an RDBMS and the query-based model of Cassandra. How would you support the query SELECT * FROM User WHERE city = 'Scottsdale' on a column family User keyed by UserID, and what does the solution cost?

Показать образец ответа

An RDBMS follows a domain-based model: you model the entities and relationships of the domain, normalise them, and answer any later question by combining tables with joins ("what answers do I have?"). Cassandra follows a query-based model: rows are found by key, column values are not queried and there are no joins, so you list the questions first and build a structure for each one ("what questions do I have?" — start from queries, then design the data model). For the query, create a second column family, UserCity, keyed by city, whose column names are the IDs of the users living there, with empty values (the Valueless Column pattern): the query becomes one row read, followed by a multiget of the users from User. This is why Cassandra is called an index factory. The cost is duplication: every user is written to both column families, and the application must keep the copies consistent, with no joins and no referential integrity to help.

Ответ держится на двух вещах: почему (строки находятся только по ключу, джойнов нет) и цена (дублирование, копии синхронизирует приложение). В CQL та же идея — таблица с PRIMARY KEY ((city), user_id). Другой инструмент с практического занятия — вторичный индекс, CREATE INDEX; задание на проектирование в экзамене 30 января 2026 года исключает его словами "Do not use indexes".

Урок 20 · Колоночные хранилища и модель данных Cassandra · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Our exercise, in the wording of the past papers: the hotel of pp. 434–435. Consider the following ER diagram. Hotel (Hotel_ID, Name, Phone, Address, City, State, Zip); Room (Room_ID, Room Number, Rate); Guest (Guest_ID, Name, Phone, Email). Relationships: Has, Hotel (1, N) – Room (1, 1); Reserve, Guest (0, N) – Room (0, N), with the attributes Start Date and End Date.

Suppose you expect to record a large-scale, continuously increasing number of reservations, and therefore you decide to store them in a columnar database. Write the specification of the tables (column families) of a Cassandra deployment so that one can query the hotels of a state, the hotels of a city of a given state, and the reservations of a guest by phone number, the most recent first. Do not use indexes.

Показать образец ответа

Two tables, one per access path: the hotels by location, partitioned by state and clustered by city, and the reservations by guest phone, partitioned by phone and clustered by start date, the most recent first. Each reservation carries copies of the guest, hotel and room data it shows, because Cassandra has no joins.

-- the keyspace (one per application)
CREATE KEYSPACE hotels
  WITH replication = {'class': 'SimpleStrategy',
                      'replication_factor': 3};
-- queries 1 and 2: the hotels of a state, or of a city in it
CREATE TABLE hotels_by_location (
  state text,
  city text,
  hotel_id text,
  name text,
  phone text,
  address text,
  zip text,
  PRIMARY KEY ((state), city, hotel_id)
);
-- query 3: a guest's reservations, with copies, newest first
CREATE TABLE reservations_by_guest_phone (
  guest_phone text,
  start_date date,
  reservation_id uuid,
  end_date date,
  guest_name text,
  hotel_id text,
  hotel_name text,
  room_number int,
  rate float,
  PRIMARY KEY ((guest_phone), start_date, reservation_id)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (start_date DESC, reservation_id ASC);
-- all the hotels of Arizona: one partition
SELECT * FROM hotels_by_location WHERE state = 'AZ';
-- the hotels of Scottsdale: the first clustering column fixed
SELECT * FROM hotels_by_location
  WHERE state = 'AZ' AND city = 'Scottsdale';
-- a guest's reservations, the most recent first
SELECT * FROM reservations_by_guest_phone
  WHERE guest_phone = '+1 480 555 0101';

Every reservation is one INSERT into reservations_by_guest_phone, with reservation_id from uuid() and the guest's name, the hotel's name and the room's number and rate copied in.

Это слайды (pp. 431–435) на CQL. hotels_by_location — это составной ключ state:city со слайда (p. 432): штат — partition key, а город — первый clustering column, так что одна таблица отвечает на оба вопроса о местоположении без диапазона ключей и без order-preserving partitioner. reservations_by_guest_phone — это Reservation со слайда (p. 435): ключ — то, по чему ищет приложение, с копиями вместо джойнов; reservation_id разделяет два бронирования одного гостя на один день, потому что INSERT с уже существующим первичным ключом перезаписывает строку. Прошлые экзамены спрашивают то же самое — задание 1 из экзамена про фермеров от 12 сентября 2025 года решено в уроке 21 вместе с правилами CQL, стоящими за каждой строкой.

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 6 Sep 2024, exercise 5 (restaurant). ER: Person (Personal_ID, Name, Surname, Birth Date, Phone Number) is the parent of Owner, Customer, Waiter and Chef (Hiring Date); Head Chef (Head Chef Since) is a Chef.

"Consider the Chef table. Write a Cassandra script to perform the operations listed below. Create the Chef table, with Personal_ID as the partition key and Phone Number as the clustering key, ordering the table based on the Phone Number. (1.5 PT) Write a CQL query to collect the chef whose name is "Riccardo". If any further operation is required, write its CQL code. (1 PT) Create a Custom data type named "contact" that contains the name, surname, and phone number of the chef (1.5 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
CREATE KEYSPACE restaurant
  WITH replication = {'class': 'SimpleStrategy',
                      'replication_factor': 3};
USE restaurant;

CREATE TABLE chef (
  personal_id text,
  phone_number text,
  name text,
  surname text,
  birth_date date,
  hiring_date date,
  PRIMARY KEY (personal_id, phone_number)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (phone_number ASC);

-- name is not part of the key: the further operation is an index
CREATE INDEX chef_name ON chef (name);
SELECT * FROM chef WHERE name = 'Riccardo';

CREATE TYPE contact (
  name text,
  surname text,
  phone_number text
);
-- optional, to use it: a list of contacts of the chef
ALTER TABLE chef ADD contacts list<frozen<contact>>;

Without the index, SELECT * FROM chef WHERE name = 'Riccardo' ALLOW FILTERING; also works, but it scans the table and is not recommended.

Три подвопроса проверяют три вещи: форму со скобками вместе с порядком кластеризации, индекс как "further operation" для неключевого столбца и определение типа. Телефонные номера — текст (ведущие + или 0 должны сохраниться). На тестовых данных Риккардо с двумя номерами вернулся двумя строками одной партиции, отсортированными по номеру: здесь порядок кластеризации что-то значит.

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 11 Feb 2025, Cassandra exercise (Formula 1). ER: Person (Personal_ID, Name, Surname, Birth_Date, Gender) is the parent of Spectator (Favourite_Team), Team Owner and Team Member, ISA (T, E).

"Consider the spectator entity. Write a CQL statement to create its corresponding Cassandra Table using Favourite_Team as a clustering key and Name as an additional partition key (1 PT)." "Write a CQL statement to return a list of all the spectators whose name is Bill, whose age is over 65, and whose favourite team is "Ferrari". Besides the query, write any additional CQL statements only if it's strictly necessary. (2 PT)" "Write the CQL command to perform a set of statements by loading them from a file located in "D:\Cassandra\Scripts\statement.txt". (1 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
CREATE TABLE spectator (
  personal_id text,
  name text,
  favourite_team text,
  surname text,
  birth_date date,
  gender text,
  PRIMARY KEY ((personal_id, name), favourite_team)
);

-- over 65 on the day of the exam (11/02/2025): born before 11/02/1960
SELECT * FROM spectator
WHERE name = 'Bill' AND favourite_team = 'Ferrari'
  AND birth_date < '1960-02-11'
ALLOW FILTERING;

SOURCE 'D:\Cassandra\Scripts\statement.txt';

No additional statement is strictly necessary. personal_id is unknown, so the partition key is incomplete, and age is a range on a column outside the key: ALLOW FILTERING is unavoidable. An index on name (allowed on one part of a composite partition key) would narrow the scan, but the query would still need ALLOW FILTERING because of the range.

Ключевое здесь — "additional partition key": name идёт во внутренние скобки рядом с personal_id. В ER нет возраста, поэтому он вычисляется из Birth_Date; оговори это допущение. Оба утверждения проверены на Cassandra 5.0.9: без ALLOW FILTERING запрос отвергается даже с индексом на name; с ним из пяти тестовых зрителей возвращается только Билл 1955 года рождения, болеющий за Ferrari. Путь можно писать и с прямыми слешами; важны кавычки.

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 30 May 2025, exercise 1 (puzzles; the paper's header prints "June 30, 2025"). ER: User (Personal ID, Name, Surname, Age, Birth Date, City, Country) — Buy (Buy Date) — Puzzle (Puzzle ID, Name, Collection Name, Number of Pieces, Width, Height, Description); Puzzle (0, N), User (0, N).

"Suppose you expect to record a large number of purchases of puzzles and you decide to store them in a columnar database. Assume you want to run typical queries by searching by the puzzle IDs and by User City or Country. Write the specification of the tables (and other artifacts if necessary) of a Cassandra deployment that stores the required data."

Показать образец ответа 
CREATE KEYSPACE puzzle_sales
  WITH replication = {'class': 'SimpleStrategy',
                      'replication_factor': 3};
USE puzzle_sales;

-- query 1: purchases of a puzzle, newest first
CREATE TABLE purchases_by_puzzle (
  puzzle_id text,
  buy_date date,
  personal_id text,
  puzzle_name text,
  user_name text,
  user_surname text,
  city text,
  country text,
  PRIMARY KEY ((puzzle_id), buy_date, personal_id)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (buy_date DESC, personal_id ASC);

-- query 2: purchases by country, or by country and city
CREATE TABLE purchases_by_location (
  country text,
  city text,
  buy_date date,
  puzzle_id text,
  personal_id text,
  puzzle_name text,
  user_name text,
  user_surname text,
  PRIMARY KEY ((country), city, buy_date, puzzle_id, personal_id)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (city ASC, buy_date DESC, puzzle_id ASC, personal_id ASC);

-- SELECT * FROM purchases_by_puzzle WHERE puzzle_id = 'PZ01';
-- SELECT * FROM purchases_by_location WHERE country = 'Italy' AND city = 'Milan';

Each purchase is written into both tables, in one BATCH. Cassandra has no joins, so the puzzle and user columns the queries return are copied into each table.

Полный ответ показывает одну таблицу на запрос, искомый атрибут как partition key, clustering key, который сохраняет уникальность строк (иначе две покупки перезапишут друг друга), и денормализованные столбцы. Город — первый clustering column, так что одна таблица обслуживает и «по стране», и «по стране и городу»; запрос только по городу отвергается (проверено) — да и название города не уникально между странами. Отдельные таблицы для города и для страны — тоже правильный ответ. Если партиция одной страны становится слишком большой, в partition key можно добавить временную корзину (time bucket), например ((country, buy_year), …).

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 12 Sep 2025, exercise 1 (farmers and local markets). ER: Product (Product ID, Name, Quantity (in grams), Expiration Date) — Sell — Market (Name and Location, both underlined, Opening Time, Closing Time, Size); Product — Buy (Buy Date, Amount) — People (Personal ID, …); every cardinality (1, N).

"Suppose you expect to record large-scale, continuously increasing number of sales of products in the market, and therefore you decide to store it in a columnar database. Write the specification of the tables (column families) of a Cassandra deployment assuming you may want to query the data by market location or product name."

Показать образец ответа 
-- a sale: who bought which product, when and how much (Buy), at which market (Sell)
CREATE TABLE sales_by_market_location (
  market_location text,
  sale_date date,
  sale_id uuid,
  market_name text,
  product_id text,
  product_name text,
  personal_id text,
  amount int,
  PRIMARY KEY ((market_location), sale_date, sale_id)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (sale_date DESC, sale_id ASC);

CREATE TABLE sales_by_product_name (
  product_name text,
  sale_date date,
  sale_id uuid,
  product_id text,
  market_name text,
  market_location text,
  personal_id text,
  amount int,
  PRIMARY KEY ((product_name), sale_date, sale_id)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (sale_date DESC, sale_id ASC);

-- SELECT * FROM sales_by_market_location WHERE market_location = 'Lodi';
-- SELECT * FROM sales_by_product_name WHERE product_name = 'Pecorino'
--   AND sale_date >= '2025-09-01';

Both tables receive every sale (one BATCH per sale); sale_id, filled with uuid(), keeps two sales of the same day apart.

Тот же шаблон, что с пазлами: два запроса, два column families, атрибут поиска как partition key, дата как clustering key, чтобы новейшие продажи шли первыми, а диапазон дат был одним срезом. "Continuously increasing" — подсказка про рост: без sale_id продажи одного дня перезаписывали бы друг друга, а для рынка, который никогда не перестаёт продавать, месячная корзина в partition key, ((market_location, sale_month), …), держит каждую партицию ограниченной.

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 30 Jan 2026, exercise 2 (hospital heating). ER: Heating System (Heater_Sys_ID, Heating_Power) is the parent of HVAC System (Hourly_KWh_Cons, Cost_Per_KWh) and Gas Heating System (Hourly_SQM_Cons, Cost_Per_SQM), ISA (T, E); Floor (Floor_ID, Number, Has_Elevator); Department (Department_ID, Specialty, N_Beds, Head_Physician_ID). Heats: Heating System (1:N) – Floor (1:N), with Start_Time, End_Time; Placed Into: Department (1:N) – Floor (1:N).

"Consider the entities Heating System (and its child entities), Floor, and Department. Define the database structure so that one can query Heaters simultaneously by Floor number and Department specialty, sorting the results by total heating power. Write the specification of the tables of a Cassandra deployment that stores the required data. Do not use indexes. (2 PT)" "Write a CQL query to collect all the Gas Heating Systems running for "cardiology" Departments. If any further operation is required, write its CQL code. (1 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
-- one row per heater, for each floor number and specialty it heats;
-- (T, E): one table with a type column, the other type's columns stay empty
CREATE TABLE heaters_by_floor_and_specialty (
  floor_number int,
  specialty text,
  heating_power float,
  heater_sys_id text,
  heater_type text,
  hourly_kwh_cons float,
  cost_per_kwh float,
  hourly_sqm_cons float,
  cost_per_sqm float,
  PRIMARY KEY ((floor_number, specialty), heating_power, heater_sys_id)
) WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY (heating_power DESC, heater_sys_id ASC);
-- SELECT * FROM heaters_by_floor_and_specialty
--   WHERE floor_number = 2 AND specialty = 'cardiology';

-- further operation for the second query: a table keyed by its conditions
CREATE TABLE heaters_by_specialty_and_type (
  specialty text,
  heater_type text,
  heater_sys_id text,
  heating_power float,
  hourly_sqm_cons float,
  cost_per_sqm float,
  hourly_kwh_cons float,
  cost_per_kwh float,
  PRIMARY KEY ((specialty, heater_type), heater_sys_id)
);
SELECT * FROM heaters_by_specialty_and_type
WHERE specialty = 'cardiology' AND heater_type = 'Gas';

"Simultaneously by Floor number and Department specialty" делает оба атрибута составным partition key; "sorting by heating power" делает мощность первым clustering column, DESC — допущение (оговори его); heater_sys_id разделяет два обогревателя равной мощности. Во втором запросе нет номера этажа, так что на первой таблице ему понадобился бы ALLOW FILTERING по всему (он выполняется, проверено); "Do not use indexes" указывает на ответ «от запроса» — вторую таблицу. ISA (T, E) превращается в столбец типа в одной таблице: пустые столбцы в Cassandra ничего не стоят.

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Explain the roles of the partition key and of the clustering key of a Cassandra table. Which conditions can the WHERE clause of a SELECT contain without ALLOW FILTERING, and why does Cassandra impose these rules?

Показать образец ответа

The primary key has two parts. The partition key, the first element (or the columns in the inner brackets), is hashed into a token that decides on which nodes the row is stored: all rows with the same partition key form one partition on the same replicas. The clustering key, the remaining columns, decides how the rows are sorted inside the partition (WITH CLUSTERING ORDER BY sets ASC or DESC). Without ALLOW FILTERING, a SELECT that restricts the partition key must restrict every one of its columns with = or IN, so Cassandra can compute the token and go to the right node. Clustering columns may be restricted only as a prefix in their declared order, with a range only on the last one, because then the result is one contiguous slice of the sorted partition; OR does not exist. Any other column needs a secondary index and an equality condition, and such a query may omit the partition key, at the price of asking every node. ALLOW FILTERING lifts the rules, but makes Cassandra read and discard rows, so its cost grows with the size of the table.

Полный ответ называет обе роли, три допустимых вида условий и причину каждого: хеш — для partition key, отсортированное хранение — для префикса clustering columns, поиск на каждом узле — для индекса. Цитировать определения со слайда (p. 1560) — безопасно.

Урок 21 · CQL: ключ, который решает всё · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Which statements about CQL are true? Select all that apply.

  • The clustering key decides on which node a row is stored. Это partition key; clustering key сортирует строки внутри партиции (p. 1560).
  • With PRIMARY KEY ((a, b), c) and no index, the clause WHERE a = 1 is rejected unless ALLOW FILTERING is added. Правило 1 со слайда (p. 1569): "WHERE clauses must include ALL components of the partition key"; половина составного ключа токена не даёт.
  • A secondary index makes range conditions (<, >) on the indexed column legal without ALLOW FILTERING. Индекс отвечает только на равенство: "most comparison operators require the additional statement ALLOW FILTERING" (p. 1568), и price > 100000 требует его и после CREATE INDEX car_price.
  • An INSERT fails if a row with the same primary key already exists. INSERT — это upsert: он перезаписывает перечисленные столбцы без ошибки.
  • With PRIMARY KEY (a, b, c) and no index, the clause WHERE a = 1 AND c = 2 is rejected unless ALLOW FILTERING is added. c ограничен, а предшествующий clustering column b — нет (правило 2).
  • A DELETE that names only the partition key removes every row of that partition. Слайд (p. 1571): clustering key "not strictly required"; без него уходит вся партиция.
Показать образец ответа

True: the second, the fifth and the sixth.

На настоящем экзамене за неправильные ответы может быть штраф (о нём объявляют прямо перед экзаменом), так что отмечай только то, что можешь обосновать. Четвёртый вариант — рефлекс из SQL; третий — самая частая ошибка про вторичные индексы.

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Describe step by step what a Cassandra replica node does when it receives a write, and explain why reads can be slower than writes.

Показать образец ответа

The replica first appends the write to the commit log on disk, a sequential append that makes it durable, and then applies it to the memtable, an in-memory structure of key-value pairs sorted by key; then it can acknowledge. No read, no disk seek and no lock are needed. When the memtable is full or old, it is flushed to disk as an SSTable (Sorted String Table): an immutable file of key-value pairs sorted by key, with an index file of (key, position) pairs and a Bloom filter. Because SSTables are never modified, new versions and deletes (tombstones) accumulate in newer SSTables, and compaction periodically merges them, keeping the newest version of each key and removing deleted items. A read, instead, must look in the memtable and possibly in several SSTables — skipping those whose Bloom filter says the key is absent — and merge the versions by timestamp; the coordinator may also contact several replicas and start a read repair. This is why reads may be slower than writes.

Полный ответ называет порядок (сначала commit log, потом memtable), слова SSTable, flush, compaction и Bloom filter и причину асимметрии. Слайд (p. 446) говорит, что чтения "touch log and multiple SSTables": пиши memtable, а лог упоминай только для перезапусков.

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

A keyspace has replication factor N = 3. Which write/read consistency-level pairs guarantee that a read returns the most recent acknowledged write? Select all that apply.

  • W = ONE, R = ONE 1 + 1 = 2 ≤ 3: чтение может спросить реплику, до которой запись ещё не дошла.
  • W = QUORUM, R = QUORUM 2 + 2 = 4 > 3: два большинства делят хотя бы одну реплику.
  • W = ANY, R = ALL ANY может удовлетворить хинт на координаторе, который сам не реплика: пока хинт не доставлен, значения нет ни у одной реплики, так что даже чтение всех трёх может его не увидеть.
  • W = ALL, R = ONE 3 + 1 = 4 > 3: значение есть у каждой реплики.
  • W = ONE, R = ALL 1 + 3 = 4 > 3: чтение спрашивает каждую реплику, включая ту, что подтвердила запись.
  • W = ONE, R = QUORUM 1 + 2 = 3, не больше 3: обе прочитанные реплики могут оказаться устаревшими.
Показать образец ответа

QUORUM/QUORUM, ALL/ONE and ONE/ALL. The condition is W + R > N (here 2 + 2, 3 + 1 and 1 + 3, all greater than 3): it forces the replicas that acknowledged the write and the replicas that answer the read to overlap in at least one node, and the coordinator returns the value with the most recent timestamp. ONE/ONE and ONE/QUORUM fail the inequality; ANY guarantees no replica at all, because a hint stored on the coordinator counts as a successful write.

Три верных варианта из шести. ANY/ALL — ловушка: похоже на угол «прочитать всё», но W считает только настоящие реплики. За неверные галочки могут штрафовать, так что отмечай только то, что доказывает неравенство.

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

True or false: a Bloom filter may report that a key is absent from an SSTable although the key is stored in it.

  • True Это был бы ложноотрицательный ответ, а фильтр Блума их никогда не даёт: биты, выставленные при вставке ключа, никогда не сбрасываются.
  • False Ложноотрицательных ответов нет; бывают только ложноположительные — отсутствующий ключ, все биты которого выставили другие ключи.
Показать образец ответа

False. When a key is inserted, all its k hashed bits are set, and bits are never reset, so a stored key always finds its bits set and the filter answers "maybe present". The filter can only err the other way: an absent key may find all its bits set by other keys (a false positive). The rate is low; the lecture's example has k = 4 hash functions, 100 items and 3200 bits, with a false-positive rate of 0.02 %.

Вопрос «верно/неверно», который проверяет направление ошибки. Пиши причину, а не только слово: "bits are never cleared".

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Explain how the nodes of a Cassandra cluster learn which nodes are alive without a master. Describe the membership list and how a node decides that another node is down.

Показать образец ответа

Cassandra is peer to peer: no master knows the membership, so each node keeps its own membership list with, for every node, its address, the latest heartbeat counter heard and the local time at which that counter was received. Each node increments its own counter and periodically gossips its list to a few peers (up to three per round), with a three-message exchange (GossipDigestSyn, GossipDigestAck, GossipDigestAck2). On receipt, for every address the node keeps the larger counter and stamps it with its own local time, because the clocks are not synchronised: node 2 at local time 70 receives 10120 and 10098 for nodes 1 and 3, larger than its 10118 and 10090, and stores (10120, 70) and (10098, 70), while it keeps its own entry and the equal one. The traffic per node stays constant and the views converge (anti-entropy). A node whose counter stops increasing becomes suspect: the accrual failure detector computes a suspicion value PHI, minus the base-10 logarithm of the probability that a heartbeat arrives later than the silence observed so far, from the past inter-arrival times; the application sets the threshold (on the slide, PHI = 5 gives a detection time of 10–15 seconds).

В полном ответе три части: нет мастера, и gossip идёт с немногими собеседниками; правило слияния с локальным временем; подозрение как величина с порогом вместо фиксированного таймаута. Числа со слайда (p. 451) делают правило слияния конкретным.

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Cassandra "prefers availability over consistency", while HBase "prefers (strong) consistency over availability". Explain where this difference comes from in the two architectures.

Показать образец ответа

In Cassandra every row has N equal replicas on a masterless ring. Any node can coordinate a write and sends it to all replicas; with levels such as ONE or ANY, and with hinted handoff, a write is accepted even while replicas are down, so the replicas may hold different versions for a while; they are reconciled later by timestamps, read repair and compaction (eventual consistency, BASE). In HBase a table is split into regions, and each region is served by exactly one HRegionServer at a time, assigned by the HMaster through ZooKeeper. Every write of a row goes through that single server, which appends it to its write-ahead log (HLog) before applying it to the MemStore, so every read of the row sees the latest write. The copies of the data are kept by HDFS at the file level, not by several servers answering for the same row. When a region server fails, its regions are unavailable until the HMaster reassigns them and the HLog is replayed into the new MemStores: HBase gives up availability during recovery to keep strong consistency.

Суть — "N equal replicas" против "one owner per region". Сам по себе write-ahead log даёт только долговечность (durability); согласованность берётся из единственного владельца. Упомяни BASE для Cassandra и недоступность во время восстановления для HBase.

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

A keyspace uses the Simple Strategy with replication factor 4. Writes are performed at consistency level QUORUM. What is the smallest number of replicas a read must wait for, so that it is guaranteed to return the latest acknowledged write?

Показать образец ответа

QUORUM with N = 4 is ⌊4/2⌋ + 1 = 3 replicas, so W = 3. A fresh read needs W + R > N, that is R > 4 − 3 = 1, so R = 2 replicas. Of the lecture's levels, ONE is not enough and QUORUM (3) is more than needed.

Два шага — размер кворума и неравенство. При N = 4 формула N/2 + 1 со слайда даёт 3 с округлением и без; при нечётном N целое число получается только с округлением вниз, так что всегда пиши floor.

Урок 22 · Внутри Cassandra: кольцо, gossip, реплики, кворумы; HBase · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

ER scenario of the exam of 12 Sep 2025 (the exercise itself is exam-style, not from the paper): a data structure for tracking farmers and their sales at local markets. Market (Name, Location, Opening Time, Closing Time, Size) and Product (Product ID, Name, Quantity, Expiration Date) are linked by the relationship Sell, with cardinality (1, N) on both sides.

The sales are stored in Cassandra, partitioned by market location, on one datacenter of six nodes n1–n6 with tokens 10, 25, 40, 55, 70 and 85; the token of the partition 'Milano' is 62. Write the CQL statement that creates the keyspace markets so that every row is stored three times. Say which nodes hold the copies of 'Milano', and choose consistency levels for writes and reads so that a read always returns the latest acknowledged sale while one replica may be down.

Показать образец ответа 
CREATE KEYSPACE markets
  WITH replication = {
    'class': 'SimpleStrategy',
    'replication_factor': 3};

With the Simple Strategy the first copy goes to the first node clockwise from the token 62, that is n5 (token 70), and the other two to the next nodes clockwise, n6 (85) and n1 (10, after wrapping around the ring). Writes and reads at QUORUM wait for ⌊3/2⌋ + 1 = 2 replicas each: 2 + 2 > 3, so every read overlaps the last acknowledged write, and either operation still succeeds with one replica down. (ONE/ALL or ALL/ONE would also be consistent, but a single replica down would block the reads or the writes.)

Строка с keyspace открывает каждый скрипт Cassandra на практическом занятии (p. 1553); здесь она соединена с правилами размещения и кворума из этого урока. Переход по кругу от n6 к n1 — то место, где ответы обычно спотыкаются.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

True or false: a data warehouse always contains the most current information available in the operational systems.

  • True Хранилище — это копия, интегрированная заранее и обновляемая загрузками: между двумя загрузками она отстаёт от источников.
  • False Первое преимущество со слайда (p. 491) идёт вместе со своей ценой: "High query performance — But not necessarily most current information".
Показать образец ответа

False. In the warehousing approach information is integrated in advance and copied into the warehouse, which is refreshed by periodic batch loads. Queries are fast and do not interfere with the sources, but the data is not necessarily the most current: it is as fresh as the last load. For rapidly changing information the query-driven approach is still better.

Ловушка — слово "always". Хорошее обоснование называет цену копирования заранее — устаревание — и случай, в котором выигрывает ленивый подход с запросами к источникам.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

According to the lecture, which of the following characterise a data warehouse (OLAP) rather than an operational OLTP database? Select all that apply.

  • Mostly reads Слайд (p. 489), колонка хранилища.
  • Many small transactions Колонка OLTP со слайда (p. 489): короткие транзакции кассы.
  • History rather than the current snapshot Слайд (p. 489): "History" против "Current snapshot"; слайд (p. 505): "Historic data".
  • Index/hash on the primary key Так OLTP находит одну строку по ключу; хранилище делает "Lots of scans".
  • Lots of scans Слайд (p. 489): аналитические запросы читают большие части данных.
  • Normalized table structure, many tables with few columns Слайд (p. 505) отдаёт это операционной системе; хранилище — "De-normalized … few tables, many columns".
  • Batch updates Слайд (p. 505): хранилище загружается батчами, у операционной системы "Continuous updates".
Показать образец ответа

Mostly reads, history, lots of scans and batch updates. Many small transactions, an index or hash on the primary key and a normalized structure with many narrow tables characterise the OLTP database.

Вопрос «выбери все верные», собранный из двух слайдов сравнения (pp. 489 и 505). Каждый вариант — строка одного из них: учи каждую строку парой, и неправильных галочек будет легко избежать.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Give Inmon's definition of a data warehouse and explain each of its four properties with an example. Then explain how the warehousing approach differs from the query-driven approach to integrating heterogeneous sources.

Показать образец ответа

Inmon defines a data warehouse as "a subject-oriented, integrated, time-varying, non-volatile collection of data that is used primarily in organizational decision making". Subject-oriented: data is organised by business subject, such as customer, product or sales, not by the application that produced it. Integrated: data from heterogeneous sources is reconciled into one consistent format and meaning. Time-varying: history is kept and every record is tied to a time — all transactions ever made at a bank, not only the current balance. Non-volatile: data is loaded in batches and then not updated, possibly append-only. In the warehousing approach information is integrated in advance: an extractor/monitor at each source feeds an integrator that loads the warehouse, and clients query the warehouse directly, with high performance and no interference with the sources, but not necessarily the most current data. In the query-driven approach (lazy, on demand) an integration system decomposes each query and sends it through wrappers to the live sources; it remains better for rapidly changing information or sources, truly vast amounts of data and clients with unpredictable needs.

В полном ответе три части: четыре прилагательных, каждое с конкретным примером, контраст «заранее против по требованию» (eager против lazy) и хотя бы по одному случаю, где выигрывает каждый подход. В книге Инмона написано "time-variant", на слайде напечатано "time-varying": пиши слово со слайда, а второе узнавай.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

A chain of pharmacies records its sales in an operational database whose ER diagram has these entities (primary key first): Sale Line (Line_ID, Date, Quantity, Amount), Medicine (Medicine_ID, Name, Category, Producer), Pharmacy (Pharmacy_ID, Name, City, Region) and Campaign (Campaign_ID, Description). Each sale line refers to exactly one medicine (1,1), exactly one pharmacy (1,1) and at most one campaign (0,1); a medicine, a pharmacy or a campaign can appear in any number of sale lines (0,N). Management wants to analyse quantities and amounts by medicine category, region and month.

(a) Design a star schema with one fact row per medicine, pharmacy, campaign and day: give the fact table with its key and its measures, and the dimension tables with their attributes, with the (min,max) cardinalities. (b) Say which tables are normalised and why. (c) Show how the Pharmacy dimension changes in a snowflake schema. (d) Write the SQL query that returns the total amount by region and month.

Показать образец ответа

(a) Fact Sale(Medicine_ID, Pharmacy_ID, Campaign_ID, Time_ID, Quantity, Amount): the key is composed of the four dimension keys, the measures are Quantity and Amount, and each row aggregates all the sale lines of that combination in that day. Dimensions: Medicine(Medicine_ID, Name, Category, Producer), Pharmacy(Pharmacy_ID, Name, City, Region), Campaign(Campaign_ID, Description), with a "no campaign" row for sales without one so that the key stays complete, and Time(Time_ID, Day, Month, Year). Every relationship is (1,1) on the Sale side and (0,N) on the dimension side.

(b) The fact table is in BCNF: its only functional dependency goes from the whole key to the measures. The dimensions are not normalised: Pharmacy_ID → City → Region and Day → Month → Year are transitive dependencies, kept on purpose because the warehouse is loaded in batches and not updated in place, and every attribute stays one join away from the fact.

(c) In a snowflake schema the hierarchy becomes separate entities: Pharmacy (1,1)—(0,N) City (1,1)—(0,N) Region, that is Pharmacy(Pharmacy_ID, Name, City_ID), City(City_ID, Name, Region_ID), Region(Region_ID, Name).

(d)

SELECT ph.Region, t.Year, t.Month, SUM(s.Amount) AS Amount
FROM Sale s
  JOIN Pharmacy ph ON s.Pharmacy_ID = ph.Pharmacy_ID
  JOIN Time t ON s.Time_ID = t.Time_ID
GROUP BY ph.Region, t.Year, t.Month;

Рассуждение о проектировании со слайдов (pp. 509–512), применённое к новому сценарию. В полном ответе есть составной ключ, меры в факте и описания в измерениях, аргумент о нормализации со слайда (p. 510) и запрос, который соединяет только нужные измерения, — с группировкой по году и месяцу, иначе апрель двух разных лет попадёт в одну группу. Строка «без кампании» — деталь, которая сохраняет ключ полным: звезда со слайда ставит Promo (1,1) у каждой продажи, а для продаж без акции нужна именно такая строка.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Consider the cube Store × Product × Time with measure Quantity. Define drill-down, roll-up, slice and dice, give an example of each, and say which clause of the SQL star query each operation changes.

Показать образец ответа

Drill-down adds one analysis dimension (disaggregation): from sales by product and quarter to sales by product, quarter and store. Roll-up removes one analysis dimension (aggregation): from sales by product, quarter and store back to sales by product and quarter, each cell being the sum over the stores; climbing a hierarchy, such as from month to year or from city to region, is also a roll-up. A slice fixes one dimension to one value and leaves a sub-cube with one dimension less, for example the sales of one product over all stores and periods. A dice selects ranges of values on several dimensions and leaves a smaller cube, for example a category of products in a region over a mid-term time span: two products, two stores and two quarters, 8 cells. In the star query, roll-up and drill-down change the GROUP BY (fewer or more grouping attributes, or a coarser or finer level of a hierarchy); slice and dice add conditions on dimension attributes to the WHERE.

Сначала дай определения со слайда (p. 513) слово в слово, потом форму с иерархией как дополнение. Связь с SQL показывает, что ты понимаешь операции, а не помнишь картинки.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Which statement describes the architecture of Snowflake?

  • Shared-disk: every compute node works on one common disk system, and all queries compete for it Половина картины: Snowflake держит одно центральное хранилище, как shared-disk, но выполняет запросы на независимых MPP-кластерах (virtual warehouses), которые друг с другом не конкурируют.
  • Shared-nothing: every node owns a partition of the data on its own disks Узлы действительно обрабатывают данные параллельно, но данными они не владеют: те лежат в одном центральном хранилище.
  • A hybrid: a central columnar storage readable by all compute nodes, independent MPP compute clusters (virtual warehouses), and a cloud services layer that coordinates them Три слоя со слайдов (pp. 524–527): Database Storage, Query Processing, Cloud Services.
  • A single large server with columnar storage, scaled by moving to a bigger machine Virtual warehouses — это MPP-кластеры из многих узлов, "allocated by Snowflake from a cloud provider" (p. 526).
Показать образец ответа

The third. Snowflake is a hybrid of the two traditional architectures: like shared-disk it keeps the data in one central repository that every compute node can access, stored in an optimized, compressed, columnar format; like shared-nothing it processes queries on MPP clusters, the virtual warehouses, which share no compute resources with each other. A cloud services layer handles authentication, infrastructure, metadata, query parsing and optimization, and access control.

Слайды рисуют две традиционные архитектуры (p. 523) и слои Snowflake (p. 524) без слова "hybrid"; документация Snowflake его использует. Вопрос проверяет, что ты можешь поставить Snowflake против обеих, а не просто назвать его слои.

Урок 23 · Data warehouses и Snowflake · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Explain the separation between compute and storage in Snowflake and why the lecture presents it as a key feature. Then explain how resizing a virtual warehouse differs from a multi-cluster warehouse, and write the statement that creates a warehouse of size XSMALL that runs between 1 and 3 clusters and suspends after 5 minutes of inactivity.

Показать образец ответа

In Snowflake the data is stored once, in a central cloud storage layer, in a compressed columnar format managed by Snowflake, while queries are processed by virtual warehouses: independent MPP compute clusters that read that storage and share no compute resources with each other. Other databases combine compute and storage, so they must be sized for the largest workload and that cost is paid all the time. With the two separated, each workload gets its own warehouse on the same data without interference, and a warehouse can be suspended when idle (AUTO_SUSPEND) and resumed automatically (AUTO_RESUME), so compute is paid only while it is used. Resizing a warehouse — scaling up, for example from XSMALL to LARGE — gives each query more compute and makes large, complex queries faster. A multi-cluster warehouse — scaling out — keeps the size but runs between MIN_CLUSTER_COUNT and MAX_CLUSTER_COUNT identical clusters, started automatically when queries queue: it serves more concurrent users but does not make a single query faster, and it requires the Enterprise Edition.

CREATE WAREHOUSE AS_WH
WITH WAREHOUSE_SIZE = 'XSMALL'
MIN_CLUSTER_COUNT = 1
MAX_CLUSTER_COUNT = 3
SCALING_POLICY = 'STANDARD'
AUTO_SUSPEND = 300
AUTO_RESUME = TRUE;

Две ловушки: слайды озаглавливают multi-cluster warehouses как "Scale Up" (p. 544), так что назови оба хода и скажи, что делает каждый; а AUTO_SUSPEND задаётся в секундах, так что пять минут — это 300.

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Which pairing of a search query with its intent type agrees with the definitions of the lecture?

  • "buy new iPhone 16 now" — commercial/investigational Пользователь готов совершить действие — купить: transactional.
  • "Facebook login page" — informational Пользователь хочет попасть на конкретный сайт: navigational.
  • "best laptop for students" — commercial/investigational Пользователь присматривается к покупке и хочет сравнить варианты или изучить товары (p. 553).
  • "How does user intent work?" — navigational Пользователь хочет что-то узнать: informational.
Показать образец ответа

The third. A user searching "best laptop for students" is considering a purchase and wants to compare options or research products: commercial/investigational intent. The other three queries are transactional ("buy new iPhone 16 now"), navigational ("Facebook login page") and informational ("How does user intent work?").

Это собственные четыре примера слайда, перемешанные. Ловушка — отличить commercial (ещё сравнивает) от transactional (готов действовать). За неправильный ответ могут снять баллы: отмечай только то, что можешь обосновать.

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

True or false: with the normed TF-IDF vectors of the lecture, ranking documents by their Euclidean distance from the query vector gives the same order as ranking them by cosine similarity.

  • True Для единичных векторов квадрат расстояния равен 2 − 2 cos θ: он уменьшается ровно тогда, когда косинус растёт.
  • False Это было бы верно для сырых векторов разной длины, где длинный документ далёк от всего; нормализация убирает длину.
Показать образец ответа

True. After normalisation every vector has length 1, so

\[ \lVert \vb{q} - \vb{d} \rVert^2 = \vb{q} \cdot \vb{q} - 2\, \vb{q} \cdot \vb{d} + \vb{d} \cdot \vb{d} \] \[ = 2 - 2\cos\theta . \]

A smaller distance means a larger cosine, so both criteria give the same ranking, and the score to compute is the dot product of the normed vectors.

Если вопрос дадут открытым, пиши однострочный вывод, а не только вердикт. Без нормализации два порядка могут различаться: длинный документ далёк от любого короткого запроса, даже если смотрит в ту же сторону.

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Consider the Dish entity of the restaurant ER diagram of the exam of 6 Sept 2024: Dish (Dish_ID, Name, Price, Recipe, Description). Three dishes are named D1 "grilled salmon", D2 "salmon tartare with avocado" and D3 "grilled octopus". Treat each name as a document (lower-case, no stemming, every word kept) and use the formulas of the lecture: TF = log(count + 1), IDF = log((#docs + 1) / #docs-the-term-occurs-in), then normalise each vector to length 1. Which dish is the most similar to D1? Enter its similarity score with three decimals.

Показать образец ответа

#docs = 3. df: grilled 2, salmon 2, tartare 1, with 1, avocado 1, octopus 1. IDF: df 2 gives log(4/2) = log 2, df 1 gives log 4 = 2 log 2. Every count is 1, so TF = log 2 for every term, and each raw weight is (log 2)² times 1 (df 2) or times 2 (df 1); the common factor cancels in the normalisation.

D1 is proportional to (grilled 1, salmon 1): 0.707 each. D2 is proportional to (salmon 1, tartare 2, with 2, avocado 2), length √13: salmon 0.277. D3 is proportional to (grilled 1, octopus 2), length √5: grilled 0.447.

sim(D1, D2) = 0.707 × 0.277 = 0.196; sim(D1, D3) = 0.707 × 0.447 = 0.316. D3 is the most similar, with 0.316.

Сценарий взят из экзамена, а вопрос — нет: ни один прошлый экзамен не просит считать TF-IDF. Покажи порядок TF, IDF, нормализация, и пусть скалярное произведение идёт только по общим терминам. У обоих соперников по одному общему слову с D1; более короткое название сохраняет больший вес на слово — снова преимущество T4 из таблицы (p. 563). Основание логарифма сокращается.

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Using the formulas of the lecture (TF = log(count + 1), IDF = log((#docs + 1) / #docs-the-term-occurs-in), then normalisation), explain why the word "data" has weight 0.316 in the title "Data Mining Your Website" but 0.187 in "Mastering Data Mining: The Art and Science of Customer Relationship Management". What would the second weight become if the word "the" were removed from that title?

Показать образец ответа

In both titles "data" occurs once, so TF = log 2, and it occurs in 3 of the 8 titles, so IDF = log(9/3) = log 3: the raw weight is the same, log 2 × log 3 = 0.7615. The difference comes from the normalisation, which divides each weight by the Euclidean length of the whole title vector: 2.408 for the four-term title, 4.074 for the eleven-term one. So 0.7615 / 2.408 = 0.316 and 0.7615 / 4.074 = 0.187: the same word counts for more in a short document. Removing "the" removes one raw weight of 1.523 from the sum under the square root: the length drops to √(16.598 − 2.320) = 3.779, and "data" rises to 0.7615 / 3.779 = 0.202, although its TF and IDF do not change.

Сказать нужно три вещи: TF тот же, IDF тот же, длина другая. Ловушка во второй части — думать, что вес "data" не может измениться, раз в самом "data" ничего не поменялось: нормализация связывает все слова документа.

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

What is an inverted index? Explain how a search engine uses it to answer a query with several terms, and why, according to the lecture, its posting lists are compressed and sorted from the most important documents to the least.

Показать образец ответа

An inverted index is the building block of a search engine: a mapping from each term (TermID) to the list of the documents that contain it (DocumentIDs), its posting list. It is called inverted because it turns the natural direction, document to words, into word to documents. To answer a query with several terms, the engine finds all documents containing the set of terms by computing a join over their posting lists — the intersection for AND, the union for OR — without reading the documents. Search engines must respond in tenths of a second, so the lists are compressed with integer compression algorithms that allow fast decompression, take the statistics of text into account and are organised in blocks for easy reading; the reason is to reduce the space in memory, so that the index is kept and read in RAM. Sorting each posting list from the most important documents (highest term counts) to the least allows early termination: the engine computes bounds on the scores that the remaining documents could reach and drops lower-ranked documents as early as possible, while index pruning removes entries that would never be retrieved for a query.

Охватить нужно четыре вещи: отображение, join, зачем сжатие (память, а значит скорость) и раннюю остановку. Если останется время, добавь скрытое допущение примера со слайда: "fox" -> только D1, потому что индекс не привёл "foxes" к основе.

Урок 24 · Поиск — это не WHERE: релевантность, TF-IDF и inverted index · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 24 June 2024, exercise 1. Consider the ER diagram representing a data structure for tracking Disney's digital products. Company Section (ID, Name, Description, Number of Employees); Digital Content (Title, Release Date, Duration, Production Cost, Minimum Age), with the sub-entities Series (Number of Episodes, …) and Movie (Sequence Number, Nominated for Award (Yes/No), …), (T, E), and Animated Movie (…), a sub-entity of Movie, (P, O); Character (Name, Nickname, Age); Actor (Personal ID, Name, Surname, Age, Birth Date, Career Description), with the sub-entity Voice Actor (Voice Tone, Voice Strength (from -5 to 5)), (P, O); Script (ID, Length, Duration, Title); Location (ID, Name, Description, Furniture (Text), Indoor (Yes/No)); Line (ID, Sequence Number, Text). Relationships: Play, Character (1:1) – Actor (1:N); Involve, Character (1:N) – Digital Content (1:N); Produce, Company Section (1:N) – Digital Content (1:N), with Starting Date and End Date; Describe, Digital Content (1:N) – Script (1:1); Include, Script (1:N) – Line (1:1); Perform, Actor (1:N) – Line (1:1), with Recording Date; Located, Script (1:N) – Location (1:1).

Which DB technology would be the best to store the entities/rel. of the ER diagram? [you may not need all the rows of the table] (1 PT) — ENTITIES / REL. | DB TYPE | MOTIVATION

Показать образец ответа
ENTITIES / REL.DB TYPEMOTIVATION
Character, Actor, Voice Actor, Line; Play, PerformGraph DB (Neo4j)Many links traversed and counted (lines an actor performed, characters an actor played); the (P, O) Voice Actor is a second label on the same node; Recording Date is an edge property.
Company Section, Digital Content, Series, Movie, Animated Movie, Script; Produce, DescribeDocument DB (MongoDB)A digital content is read as one aggregate with its scripts embedded (each script describes exactly one content); the sub-entities have different attributes, which schema-less documents hold without empty fields; Produce, with its dates, is a sub-collection of each content.

Его задание 4 просит найти Locations, в description которых есть "Stadium" и "Basketball", "assigning a higher score" тем, у кого в furniture есть "Planet", а в description — "Space": это ранжирование, а не условие WHERE. Именно такую мотивацию и должна дать третья строка — свободный текст, инвертированный индекс, оценка релевантности.

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 11 Feb 2025, Elasticsearch exercise, first sub-question. ER scenario: a data structure for tracking Formula 1 races. Person (Personal_ID, Name, Surname, Birth_Date, Gender) has the children Spectator, Team Owner and Team Member, (T, E); Team Member has the children Engineer, Mechanic and Pilot, (P, E). Pilot - (...), Championships_Won, Career (Textual). The second sub-question will ask for male pilots who won at least 10 championships and whose career must include the words "Champion" and "Car".

Consider the Pilot entity. Provide the complete mapping of the index for the considered entity (i.e., field name, field type, the structure of the mapping, etc.) (1 PT)

Показать образец ответа 
PUT /pilots
{
  "mappings": {
    "properties": {
      "personal_id": { "type": "keyword" },
      "name": { "type": "text" },
      "surname": { "type": "text" },
      "birth_date": { "type": "date", "format": "yyyy-MM-dd" },
      "gender": { "type": "keyword" },
      "championships_won": { "type": "integer" },
      "career": { "type": "text" }
    }
  }
}

A pilot is a person and a team member, so its documents carry the inherited attributes as well. The identifier and the gender are keywords (exact match), names and career are full text, the number of championships is an integer and the birth date a date with its format.

В экзамене напечатано "Career (Textual), Career (Textual)" — достаточно одного поля. Вопрос проверяет структуру mappings → properties → по записи на атрибут и типы, подходящие под следующие запросы: "male" — точное значение gender, "at least 10" — диапазон по целому числу, "Champion" и "Car" — слова в текстовом поле. Если забыть унаследованные атрибуты, у следующего запроса не будет gender, по которому фильтровать. Этот запрос решается в уроке 26.

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

People (Personal ID, Name, Surname, Age, Address, State) has the child Farmer, (P, O), with the attributes Biography and Experienced (Yes/No). Farmer (1, 2) — Work — (1, N) Farm; Farmer (1, N) — Cultivate — (1, N) Crop.

Consider the Farmer entity. Provide the complete mapping of the index for the considered entity (i.e., field name, field type, the structure of the mapping, etc.) (1 PT)

Показать образец ответа 
PUT /farmers
{

    "properties": {
      "personal_id": { "type": "keyword" },
      "name": { "type": "text" },
      "surname": { "type": "text" },
      "age": { "type": "integer" },
      "address": { "type": "text" },
