Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

An index of restaurant inspections answers the request below with "hits" : [ ], although many of its documents contain "BORO" : "Queens". Which explanation is correct?

GET /nyc-restaurants/_search
{ "query": { "match": { "BORO": "queens" } } }
  • A match query is always case-sensitive. На поле text с анализатором standard обе стороны переводятся в нижний регистр, и "queens" находит "Queens".
  • The index has a single shard, so only part of the documents is searched. Поиск доходит до каждого шарда индекса; если шард один, в нём лежат все документы.
  • A match query cannot be used on fields that hold strings. Тот же match с "Queens" возвращает попадания (p. 597).
  • BORO is mapped as keyword, which has no analyzer, so the query term "queens" must equal the stored term "Queens" exactly. Слова слайда (p. 598): "Borough (BORO) was mapped as a keyword. A keyword does not have any analyser!"
Показать образец ответа

BORO is mapped as keyword. A keyword field has no analyzer: the stored value "Queens" is indexed as one exact term, and the query text "queens" is also used as one exact term. The two terms differ in case, so no document matches. With a text field and the standard analyzer, both would be lower-cased and the query would match.

Закрытый вопрос по слайдам (pp. 597–598). За неправильные отметки могут снять баллы: соблазнительный неверный ответ — первый, который верен только для полей keyword и для анализаторов, не переводящих в нижний регистр.

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Explain the difference between primary shards and replica shards in Elasticsearch. How are write and read operations routed, and which of the two makes an index resistant to the failure of a node?

Показать образец ответа

An index is partitioned into primary shards: each document belongs to exactly one primary shard, and the shards are distributed over the nodes of the cluster, so the data and the work are spread over several machines. A replica shard is a copy of a primary shard, always stored on a different node from its primary. A write operation is performed on the primary shard and then copied to its replicas, so all copies stay identical; a read operation can be routed to the primary or to any replica, so replicas also increase the capacity to serve reads. Resistance to faults comes from the replicas: when a node fails, a replica of each primary that was on it is promoted to primary and no data is lost, while without replicas the shards of the failed node are lost. Both numbers are set per index with number_of_shards and number_of_replicas.

Слайды (p. 578) относят устойчивость к отказам к шардам; занятие (p. 1621) повторяет это и считает реплики одним из "two types of shards". Ответ, который называет механизм — копию на другом узле, — верен при любом прочтении и показывает, что ты понимаешь почему.

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

True or false: once an index contains documents, the type of one of its existing fields can be changed in place with PUT /index_name/_mapping.

  • True Термины этого поля проиндексированы под старый тип. PUT /index_name/_mapping может добавить новые поля, но не может сменить тип существующего.
  • False Чтобы сменить тип, создают новый индекс с новым маппингом и переиндексируют в него документы.
Показать образец ответа

False. The mapping of an existing field cannot be changed once documents are indexed: to change its type, create a new index with the correct mapping and reindex the data. PUT /index_name/_mapping can add new fields, but it cannot retype an existing one.

Утверждение ложно и по напечатанной фразе слайда (p. 580), и по точному правилу — безопасно в обоих случаях. Если бы оно звучало как "No field can be added to an index that has documents", формулировка слайда и факты разошлись бы (на слайде (p. 588) поле добавляют).

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Which request lets Elasticsearch generate the identifier of the new document, so that sending it twice stores two documents?

  • PUT /blog/_doc/1 Создаёт или заменяет документ с id 1: если отправить дважды, в индексе всё равно один документ, с _version 2.
  • GET /blog/_doc/1 GET читает документ; он ничего не сохраняет.
  • POST /blog/_doc "POST doesn't require the ID of the resource → duplicates" (p. 583): каждый вызов получает новый сгенерированный _id.
  • PUT /blog Создаёт индекс blog, а не документ.
Показать образец ответа

POST /blog/_doc. Without an id, Elasticsearch creates and assigns one, so repeating the request creates a duplicate. PUT /blog/_doc/1 requires the id and creates or updates that same document.

Пара POST /index/_doc против PUT /index/_doc/id — это весь слайд (p. 583); два других варианта проверяют, умеешь ли ты читать глагол и endpoint.

Урок 25 · Внутри индекса Elasticsearch — и остальной ELK · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Describe the logical flow of data inside a Logstash pipeline. What are the queue and the workers for, and which delivery guarantee does Logstash offer?

Показать образец ответа

A Logstash pipeline receives events from one or more inputs, for example Beats, TCP, UDP or HTTP listeners, or a JDBC input that polls a database. All inputs write into a single queue that buffers the events, either in memory or persistently on disk; the queue decouples the inputs from the processing and absorbs bursts. Worker threads take batches of events from the queue and run them through the filters, which structure, transform, normalize and enrich the events (for example mutate or geoip), and then through the outputs, for example Elasticsearch. Several workers run in parallel, which gives server-side scalability. Codecs attached to inputs and outputs deserialize and serialize the data. Logstash offers at-least-once delivery: in most conditions an event is delivered exactly once, but after an unclean shutdown it may be delivered twice; this requires the persistent queue. Events that cannot be processed go to the dead letter queue, so that they are not lost and the pipeline does not halt.

Скелет ответа — рисунок со слайда (p. 624): Input → Queue → Worker (Filter → Output). Если назвать два вида очереди и условие at least once, видно, что ты знаешь, почему гарантия выполняется, а не только как она называется.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 6 Jun 2024, exercise 4 — Elasticsearch (4 PT). ER (tracking natural disasters), the part used: Location (Postal Code, City, Country, Description); Occurr: Location 0:N – Natural Disaster 1:N.

"Consider the Location entity. 4.1. Provide the complete mapping of the index (i.e., field name, field type, the structure of the mapping, etc.) (1 PT) PUT …"

"Write a query to return the list of all the locations whose description includes the words "Beautiful" and "Town", removing the ones whose name includes the word "Milano". The condition on the word "Town" should not affect the final score. (1.5 PT)"

"Write a query to return the count of the locations for each country. (1.5 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
PUT /locations
{
  "mappings": {
    "properties": {
      "postal_code": { "type": "keyword" },
      "city": { "type": "text" },
      "country": { "type": "keyword" },
      "description": { "type": "text" }
    }
  }
}
GET /locations/_search
{
  "query": {
    "bool": {
      "must": [
        { "match": { "description": "Beautiful" } }
      ],
      "filter": [
        { "match": { "description": "Town" } }
      ],
      "must_not": [
        { "match": { "city": "Milano" } }
      ]
    }
  }
}
GET /locations/_search
{
  "size": 0,
  "aggs": {
    "locations_per_country": {
      "terms": { "field": "country" }
    }
  }
}

Location has no Name attribute: I assume the paper means the city.

Эту задачу решают три выбора. Почтовый индекс и страна — keyword: один сравнивают целиком, по другой считают, — а описание — text. "Town … should not affect the final score" отправляет это слово в filter, где match по-прежнему анализируется, но ничего не оценивает; "Beautiful" остаётся в must. Readme к экзаменам предупреждает, что задача может назвать то, чего нет на её диаграмме, и велит "consider all the necessary assumptions to solve the exercise": запиши своё допущение одной строкой, как выше. Добавь "size": 250 внутрь terms, если должна появиться каждая страна, а не только десять крупнейших.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 24 Jun 2024, exercise 4 — Elasticsearch (4 PT). ER (tracking Disney's digital products), the part used: Location (ID, Name, Description, Furniture (Text), Indoor (Yes/No)); Located: Script 1:N – Location 1:1.

"Consider the Location entity. 4.1. Provide the complete mapping of the index (i.e., field name, field type, the structure of the mapping, etc.) (1 PT) PUT …"

"Write a query to return the list of Locations whose description includes the words "Stadium" and "Basketball", assigning a higher score to those including the words "Planet" in their furniture and "Space" in their description. (1.5 PT)"

"Write a query to return the count of indoor and outdoor locations. (1.5 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
PUT /locations
{
  "mappings": {
    "properties": {
      "id": { "type": "keyword" },
      "name": { "type": "text" },
      "description": { "type": "text" },
      "furniture": { "type": "text" },
      "indoor": { "type": "boolean" }
    }
  }
}
GET /locations/_search
{
  "query": {
    "bool": {
      "must": [
        { "match": { "description": "Stadium" } },
        { "match": { "description": "Basketball" } }
      ],
      "should": [
        { "match": { "furniture": "Planet" } },
        { "match": { "description": "Space" } }
      ]
    }
  }
}
GET /locations/_search
{
  "size": 0,
  "aggs": {
    "indoor_and_outdoor": {
      "terms": { "field": "indoor" }
    }
  }
}

The aggregation returns two buckets, one for true (indoor) and one for false (outdoor), each with its doc_count; a boolean bucket prints its key as 1 or 0, with "key_as_string" "true" or "false".

Indoor — атрибут Yes/No: boolean, и у его агрегации terms ровно два бакета. "Includes the words "Stadium" and "Basketball"" требует обоих слов: две клаузы match в must или одна с "operator": "and" — компактный match с обоими словами принял бы любое из них. Два бонуса стоят на двух разных полях, значит, это две клаузы should: один match не может назвать два поля.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 6 Sep 2024, exercise 4 — Elasticsearch (4 PT). ER (a restaurant), the part used: Dish (Dish_ID, Name, Price, Recipe, Description); Cook: Dish 1:N – Chef 1:N; Use: Dish 1:N – Ingredient 1:N.

"Consider the Dish entity. 4.1. Provide the complete mapping of the index (i.e., field name, field type, the structure of the mapping, etc.) (1 PT) PUT …"

"Write a query to return the list of all the dishes whose description and name include the word "salmon", prioritising the one whose recipe includes the words "stir" or "boil". (1.5 PT)"

"Write a query to return the list of all the dishes whose price exceeds 10.00€, whose name is "focaccia ligure", and whose descriptions do not include the word "butter". The condition on the description attribute must not affect the final score. (1.5 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
PUT /dishes
{
  "mappings": {
    "properties": {
      "dish_id": { "type": "keyword" },
      "name": { "type": "text" },
      "price": { "type": "double" },
      "recipe": { "type": "text" },
      "description": { "type": "text" }
    }
  }
}
GET /dishes/_search
{
  "query": {
    "bool": {
      "must": [
        { "match": { "description": "salmon" } },
        { "match": { "name": "salmon" } }
      ],
      "should": [
        { "match": { "recipe": "stir boil" } }
      ]
    }
  }
}
GET /dishes/_search
{
  "query": {
    "bool": {
      "must": [
        { "range": { "price": { "gt": 10.00 } } },
        {
          "match": {
            "name": {
              "query": "focaccia ligure",
              "operator": "and"
            }
          }
        }
      ],
      "must_not": [
        { "match": { "description": "butter" } }
      ]
    }
  }
}

Запрос 1: "description and name include the word" — это два поля, значит, две клаузы match в must; "stir" или "boil" — один match, по умолчанию OR, в should. Запрос 2: условие на описание — отрицание, а must_not никогда не оценивает — это уже выполняет "must not affect the final score". Если name замаплен как text, "name is "focaccia ligure"" — это match с "operator": "and"; замапь ты его как keyword, это был бы term по точному сохранённому значению, с заглавными буквами. Что бы ты ни выбрал, запрос должен соответствовать твоему собственному маппингу.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 13 Jun 2025, exercise 5 — Elasticsearch (3 PT). (The paper's header misprints its date as "Jan. 21, 2025".) ER (tracking concert attendances and performances), the part used: Track (Title, Duration (minutes), Composer (e.g., Mozart), Music Type (e.g., classical)); Perform: Orchestra (0,N) – Track (1,N).

"Consider the Track entity. Provide the complete mapping of the index for the considered entity (i.e., field name, field type, the structure of the mapping, etc.) (1 PT) PUT /"

"Write a query to return the documents whose composer is "Bach", whose duration is greater than 8 minutes, and whose music type is classical, prioritizing those with duration greater than 11 minutes. The condition on the composer must not affect the final score (2 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
PUT /tracks
{
  "mappings": {
    "properties": {
      "title": { "type": "text" },
      "duration": { "type": "integer" },
      "composer": { "type": "keyword" },
      "music_type": { "type": "keyword" }
    }
  }
}
GET /tracks/_search
{
  "query": {
    "bool": {
      "must": [
        { "range": { "duration": { "gt": 8 } } },
        { "term": { "music_type": "classical" } }
      ],
      "filter": [
        { "term": { "composer": "Bach" } }
      ],
      "should": [
        { "range": { "duration": { "gt": 11 } } }
      ]
    }
  }
}

Композитор идёт в filter, как требует задание. Порог в 8 минут и тип музыки обязательны: must (в filter они тоже были бы верны — задание запрещает оценивать только композитора). range на 11 минут в should добавляет 1.0 длинным трекам, и поэтому они идут первыми: "prioritizing". Композитор и тип музыки — keyword, чтобы term находил "Bach" и "classical" целиком; с текстовыми полями ты написал бы match.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 12 Sep 2025, exercise 3, second sub-question — Elasticsearch (2 PT). ER (tracking farmers and their sales at local markets), the part used: People (Personal ID, Name, Surname, Age, Address, State) and its child Farmer ((…), Biography, Experienced (Yes/No)), ISA (P, O); Cultivate: Farmer (1, N) – Crop (1, N).

The first sub-question, the complete mapping of the Farmer index (1 PT), is solved in lesson 25; this one uses its field names.

"Write a query to return the farmers whose name is "Bill", whose biography contains the words "cactus" and "aloe", and does not contain the word "potato". Additionally, if the "crop" word is found, assign a higher score to the farmer. (2 PT)"

Показать образец ответа 
GET /farmers/_search
{
  "query": {
    "bool": {
      "must": [
        { "match": { "name": "Bill" } },
        { "match": { "biography": "cactus" } },
        { "match": { "biography": "aloe" } }
      ],
      "must_not": [
        { "match": { "biography": "potato" } }
      ],
      "should": [
        { "match": { "biography": "crop" } }
      ]
    }
  }
}

Каждое условие на слова стоит на текстовом поле, отсюда match: "contains the words "cactus" and "aloe"" — оба обязательны, две клаузы в must; "does not contain" — must_not; "if the "crop" word is found, assign a higher score" — should. Маппинг имени как text позволяет "Bill" совпасть в любом регистре.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Explain the difference between query context and filter context in Elasticsearch. Which clauses of a bool query run in each context, and when would you place a condition in filter context?

Показать образец ответа

In query context a clause answers the question "how well does this document match?": it decides whether the document matches and also computes a relevance score, and the hits are sorted by that score. A leaf query placed directly under query, and the must and should clauses of a bool query, run in query context. In filter context a clause answers only "does this document match?": it decides membership, but no score is computed. The filter and must_not clauses of a bool query run in filter context. A condition belongs in filter context when it must hold but should not influence the ranking — an exact category, a date range, or any condition that the task says "must not affect the final score". Filtering is also cheaper: no scoring is done, and the yes/no result can be cached and reused by later queries.

Полный ответ называет два вопроса (насколько хорошо / подходит ли), говорит, что в одном оценка считается, а в другом нет, раскладывает все четыре клаузы — must_not тоже работает в контексте фильтра, и об этом часто забывают, — и даёт причину фильтровать: ранжирование против чистого условия, плюс кэш.

Урок 26 · Запросы к Elasticsearch: match, filter, bool и агрегации · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

A bool query with a must clause (a range on the inspection date) and a must_not clause returns 8923 hits, all with score 1.0. A should clause, a term on the borough "Queens", is added. Which statement describes the new response?

  • Fewer hits: only the inspections in Queens. Так поступил бы второй must или filter. Рядом с must клауза should необязательна.
  • More hits: those in the date range or in Queens. Клауза should не может впустить документы, не прошедшие must: каждый хит по-прежнему обязан попасть в диапазон.
  • The same 8923 hits; the Queens inspections get a higher score and move to the top. Когда есть must, should только добавляет свою оценку подошедшим под неё документам: лекция печатает 8923 хита и лучшую оценку 2.487945 (p. 606).
  • The same hits with the same scores. Подошедшая клауза should "will contribute to the score" (p. 1653): инспекции из Queens получают по 1.4879451 каждая.
Показать образец ответа

The same 8923 hits. Because the query has a must clause, the should clause is optional: it removes and adds no document, but it adds its score to the inspections in Queens, which rise to the top (best score 1.0 + 1.4879451 = 2.487945).

Это собственный пример лекции (pp. 604–606). Слайд подписывает should как "OR", и ловушка — ответ «больше хитов». На экзамене за неверные закрытые ответы могут снимать баллы, так что если сомневаешься, вспомни: should — это OR, только когда она одна.

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Which of the following statements are true? Select all that apply.

  • Cosine similarity always takes values between 0 and 1. Неверно: оно меняется от −1 (противоположные векторы) через 0 (ортогональные) до 1 (одно направление) (p. 655). В пределах [0, 1] остаются только векторы без отрицательных компонент.
  • Locality-sensitive hashing differs from conventional hashing in that hash collisions are maximized, not minimized. Верно, почти дословно со слайда (p. 667): похожие объекты должны попадать в один бакет.
  • A K-D tree answers nearest-neighbour queries in O(log n) whatever the dimensionality of the vectors. Неверно: только при гипотезе \(n \gg 2^k\) (p. 661); на высокоразмерных данных "the efficiency is no better than exhaustive search".
  • If all vectors are normalised to unit length, ranking by Euclidean distance gives the same order as ranking by cosine similarity. Верно: на единичных векторах \(\norm{A-B}^2 = 2 - 2\cos\theta\) — убывающая функция косинуса.
  • HNSW guarantees to return the exact nearest neighbour. Неверно: "HNSW does not guarantee an exact nearest neighbor search" (p. 670); жадная прогулка может остановиться в локальном минимуме.
  • In IVFPQ, the vector reconstructed from its product-quantization code is identical to the original vector. Неверно: "the reconstructed vector is not identical to the original vector" (p. 674); квантование теряет информацию.
Показать образец ответа

True: the second and the fourth. Cosine similarity ranges from −1 to 1; the O(log n) of a K-D tree holds only when n ≫ 2^k; HNSW and IVFPQ are approximate methods, and product quantization loses information.

Когда за неверные ответы возможен штраф, отмечай только то, что подкреплено фразой со слайда. Каждое неверное утверждение здесь искажает одну такую фразу: неверный диапазон, выброшенное условие ("whatever the dimensionality") или «приближённый», превращённый в «точный».

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Compute the cosine similarity between the vectors \(A = (2, 1)\) and \(B = (0, 2)\), to three decimals. Are the two vectors similar, orthogonal or opposite?

Показать образец ответа

\(A\cdot B = 2\cdot 0 + 1\cdot 2 = 2\); \(\norm{A} = \sqrt{5} \approx 2.236\); \(\norm{B} = 2\).

\[ \cos\theta = \frac{A\cdot B}{\norm{A}\,\norm{B}} = \frac{2}{2.236 \cdot 2} \approx 0.447 \]

The angle is about 63°: the vectors are moderately similar. The cosine is positive, so they are not opposite (cosine −1), and it is clearly above 0, so they are not orthogonal, but it is well below 1, the value of vectors pointing the same way.

Векторы из ноутбука занятия Comparison, который печатает 0.4472. Обычные промахи: забыть квадратный корень в норме или разделить на сумму длин вместо их произведения.

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Explain what an embedding is and why a vector database can retrieve documents that a keyword search based on an inverted index would miss. Use the query "Rainy days make me sad" as an example.

Показать образец ответа

Vectorization or embedding is the process of converting data, such as text or images, into dense numerical vectors that capture their semantics, using an embedding model: an encoder (BERT for text, CNNs or ViTs for images, CLIP for both) trained with metric learning so that similar items are close together and dissimilar ones far apart. A vector database stores these high-dimensional vectors; a query is embedded with the same model, and the database returns the stored vectors most similar to it, typically by cosine similarity, which compares the directions of the vectors. An inverted index only finds documents that contain the query's terms. "Colds are unpleasant." shares no word with "Rainy days make me sad", yet in the lecture's example its cosine similarity with the query is 0.419, the second best after "Rainy days can be gloomy." (0.764), because both sentences express discomfort. Vector search is contextual search that goes beyond keyword matching.

Полный ответ состоит из четырёх частей: определение, роль модели (смысл создаёт она; база данных хранит и сравнивает), мера сходства и контраст с поиском по ключевым словам — с примером, где нет общих слов.

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Why do vector databases not rely on K-D trees or R-trees to find the nearest neighbours of embedding vectors? What do they use instead, and at what price?

Показать образец ответа

A K-D tree is a binary tree whose nodes split the space with axis-aligned hyperplanes at the median, cycling through the dimensions. Its search costs O(log n) only under the hypothesis n ≫ 2^k, where k is the dimensionality; embeddings have hundreds of dimensions (2^384 ≈ 3.9 × 10^115), so the hypothesis never holds, and a nearest-neighbour search must examine most of the tree: "the efficiency is no better than exhaustive search". An R-tree groups nearby objects into minimum bounding rectangles and skips every subtree whose rectangle the query does not intersect; in high dimension the rectangles overlap and cover much empty space, so little can be skipped. This is the curse of dimensionality. Vector databases therefore use Approximate Nearest Neighbor algorithms — LSH, HNSW, IVFPQ — which are much faster but may miss the true nearest neighbour: they sacrifice a small amount of accuracy for speed.

Полный ответ называет гипотезу \(n \gg 2^k\) и её смысл, фразу "curse of dimensionality" и компромисс ANN. Если назвать правило отсечения R-дерева, видно, что ты понимаешь, что именно перестаёт работать.

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Compare HNSW and IVFPQ: how each organizes the vectors, how a query is answered, and what each trades for speed.

Показать образец ответа

HNSW builds a navigable small-world graph in which every vector is a node linked to its near neighbours, organized in layers, each with fewer nodes than the layer below; layer 0 contains all the vectors. The top layer of a new vector is chosen at random, and the vector is linked to its closest neighbours in that layer and in the layers below. A query starts at the entry point in the top layer, moves greedily to the linked node closest to the query, and descends a layer whenever no neighbour is closer, until layer 0. It needs few comparisons but stores the full vectors plus the links, and the result is approximate. IVFPQ first partitions the space with k-means (coarse quantization): every vector is filed in the inverted list of its nearest coarse centroid. Product quantization then compresses each vector by cutting it into sub-vectors and replacing each with the id of its nearest centroid in a small codebook. A query first finds the nearest centroids and searches only those clusters (coarse search), then compares itself with the compressed codes inside them (refined search). It is memory-efficient and fast but approximate twice, since the true neighbour may sit in a cluster that was not searched and distances computed on codes are estimates; its accuracy is tuned through the number of clusters searched and the precision of the quantization.

Хороший ответ ведёт два индекса параллельно — структура, поиск, компромисс. И обходит два промаха слайдов: верхние слои HNSW случайны, а не "representative points", и HNSW быстрый, а не компактный.

Урок 27 · Смысл как координаты: embeddings, сходство и поиск соседей · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

An exercise in the style of the past papers' "Which DB technology" table. Consider the ER model of a system tracking Formula 1 races (the scenario of the Exam of 11 Feb 2025): Person (Personal_ID, Name, Surname, Birth_Date, Gender) with the subtypes Spectator (Favourite_Team), Team Member and Team Owner (Total_Assets, Biography (Textual)), an ISA hierarchy (T, E); Team Member has the subtypes Engineer and Mechanic (Role_Description (Textual)) and Pilot (Championships_Won, Career (Textual)), an ISA hierarchy (P, E); Team, Race, Track and Ticket complete the model.

The organizers want a "find similar pilots" feature: given a free-text description such as "a young driver who wins on wet tracks", return the pilots whose career is most similar in meaning, even when it uses different words. Which DB technology would be the best to store the entities and attributes involved? Fill in one row of the table ENTITIES / REL. · DB TYPE · MOTIVATION, and say what is stored for each pilot.

Показать образец ответа
ENTITIES / REL.DB TYPEMOTIVATION
Pilot (Career), and in the same way Team Owner (Biography), Engineer and Mechanic (Role_Description)Vector database (e.g. Milvus / Zilliz)The textual attributes are converted by an embedding model into dense vectors that capture their meaning; the database indexes them (e.g. with HNSW) and returns the k vectors most similar to the embedded description, by cosine similarity: semantic search beyond keyword matching.

For each pilot: Personal_ID, Name and Championships_Won as scalar fields (to identify and filter the results), the Career text or a reference to it, and the embedding of the Career (for example 384 numbers with all-MiniLM-L6-v2). The description in the query is embedded with the same model.

Упражнение наше; сама таблица стоит 1 балл на экзаменах 6 Jun 2024, 24 Jun 2024, 6 Sep 2024, 21 Jan 2025 и 13 Jun 2025 (на последнем в шапке напечатано "Jan. 21, 2025"). Хорошая строка называет одно семейство БД и даёт мотивацию, привязанную к данным или к запросу. Для текста настоящий выбор — ключевые слова против смысла: условие вроде "the career includes the words Champion and Car", задача по Elasticsearch с того же экзамена 11 Feb 2025, — это работа для поискового движка; «похожий по смыслу» — для векторной базы данных.

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски»

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Exam of 11 Feb 2025, exercise 2 — Vector DB (1 PT). ER (tracking Formula 1 races), the part used: Pilot, a child of Team Member (P, E), itself a child of Person (T, E) — Person (Personal_ID, Name, Surname, Birth_Date, Gender); Pilot adds Championships_Won and Career (Textual).

"Complete the following Zilliz query that considers the pilots whose name starts with "Mic", and computes how many pilots won each given number of championships. The results will be in the form: (NumberOfWonChampionships, NumberOfWinningPilots). Return only the first 5 results."

pilots = client.search (
    collection_name = "pilot_collection",
    data = [...],
    search_params = {"metric_type": "IP", "params": {"level": 1}},
    ___________ = _____________________________________________
    ___________ = _____________________________________________
    ___________ = _____________________________________________
)
Показать образец ответа 
    filter = 'name like "Mic%"',
    group_by_field = "championships_won",
    limit = 5

The filter keeps the pilots whose name starts with "Mic" (like with the % wildcard), group_by_field returns one result for each distinct number of championships won, and limit = 5 keeps the first five groups. A grouping search returns one representative pilot per value, not the number of pilots: the count per value would need client.query with output_fields = ["count(*)"] and a filter on each value.

Что проверяющий, скорее всего, ищет: три имени аргументов так, как их пишет pymilvus, шаблон like с % внутри Python-строки, имена полей, совпадающие с коллекцией, и 5. Схема оценивания не публикуется, так что оговорка в одно предложение — дешёвая страховка: она показывает, что ты знаешь, что группировка возвращает одного пилота на значение, а не подсчёт, о котором просит текст. Другая строка, которую может иметь в виду проверяющий, — output_fields = ["championships_won"], которая печатает значение рядом с каждым попаданием; но вместо group_by_field она ничего не группирует, а вместо limit игнорирует "Return only the first 5 results". Фильтр, группировка и limit один к одному соответствуют трём предложениям.

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

In the style of the exam of 11 Feb 2025. ER (tracking Formula 1 races), the part used: Team Owner, a child of Person (T, E) — Person (Personal_ID, Name, Surname, Birth_Date, Gender); Team Owner adds Total_Assets and Biography (Textual). The collection owner_collection stores the embedding of the biography as its vector field.

Complete the following Zilliz query that returns the name and the total assets of the 3 team owners whose biography is most similar to the given vector, considering only the owners whose total assets exceed 1000000.

owners = client.search (
    collection_name = "owner_collection",
    data = [...],
    search_params = {"metric_type": "IP", "params": {"level": 1}},
    ___________ = _____________________________________________
    ___________ = _____________________________________________
    ___________ = _____________________________________________
)
Показать образец ответа 
    filter = "total_assets > 1000000",
    output_fields = ["name", "total_assets"],
    limit = 3

The filter restricts the candidates to the owners with total assets above 1000000 before the ranking, output_fields returns the name and the total assets of each hit, and limit = 3 keeps the three most similar biographies.

Те же три пропуска, заполненные иначе: сравнение вместо like, output_fields — потому что текст говорит, что вернуть, и limit для "the 3 … most similar". Само сходство уже заложено в data и search_params: никогда не помещай векторное условие в фильтр.

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

True or false: "FAISS is a vector database."

  • True У FAISS нет ни сервера, ни контроля доступа, ни репликации, ни метаданных: слайды относят его к "Libraries for vector search and comparisons" (p. 678).
  • False Это библиотека, которая индексирует векторы и ищет по ним внутри твоей программы; векторная база данных добавляет распределённость, масштабируемость, контроль доступа, отказоустойчивость и мониторинг (p. 696).
Показать образец ответа

False. FAISS is a library for efficient similarity search and clustering of dense vectors: it stores vectors in an index in memory and returns the ids and distances of the nearest neighbours. A vector database such as Pinecone or Milvus wraps an index with distribution, scalability, access control, fault tolerance and monitoring.

За неверный ответ в «верно/неверно» может быть штраф, так что причина важна ещё до клика: «библиотека» против «базы данных» — ровно та граница, что проведена на слайдах (p. 678) и (p. 696).

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

In FAISS, an IndexIVFFlat is built with nlist = 100 and searched first with nprobe = 1, then with nprobe = 10. Explain what nlist and nprobe are, how search time and accuracy change when nprobe grows, and which of the two can be changed without rebuilding the index.

Показать образец ответа

nlist is the number of Voronoi cells into which IndexIVFFlat partitions the space: training runs k-means on the database vectors to place nlist centroids, and each vector is stored in the list of its nearest centroid. nprobe is the number of cells, out of nlist, that a search visits: the query is compared with the centroids, and only the vectors of the nprobe nearest cells are compared with it; the default is 1. The cost per query is about nlist + nprobe × nb / nlist distance computations, so the search time grows roughly linearly with nprobe, while the accuracy — the recall, the share of the true nearest neighbours that are found — rises towards that of exact search. Only nprobe can change without rebuilding: it is an attribute set between searches, whereas a new nlist means new centroids, a new training and adding every vector again.

Баллы приносят три вещи: два определения, направление компромисса (больше проб — больше времени, выше recall) и то, какая ручка — настройка времени поиска. Слайд называет оба "parameters to the search method"; ясно скажи, что nlist фиксируется при обучении. Число делает компромисс конкретным, если ты его помнишь: на коде слайда recall@4 растёт от 0.654 с одной пробой до 1.000 с десятью (посчитано для этой тетради, на слайдах не напечатано).

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Explain the difference between a vector index such as FAISS and a vector database such as Pinecone or Milvus. Describe the scatter-gather pattern and explain why every shard must return k results.

Показать образец ответа

A vector index such as FAISS is a library: it keeps vectors in memory inside a program and returns the nearest neighbours of a query, with ids that are row numbers; there is no server, no metadata, and persistence is a file. A vector database wraps an index with the capabilities production needs — distribution, scalability, access control, fault tolerance and monitoring — and offers an API, persistence and post-processing of the results, such as re-ranking and filters. Its data is sharded across nodes. Since no key tells which shard holds a query's neighbours, the query is sent to all shards (scatter), each shard returns its local top k, and the results are merged into the global top k (gather). Each shard must return k results because a vector of the global top k can be anywhere in the top k of its own shard; with fewer results per shard a true neighbour could be lost. Replication keeps copies of the data on other nodes, so a failed node is replaced.

Назови хотя бы четыре из пяти возможностей словами слайда, опиши scatter-gather как два хода и объясни, почему каждый шард возвращает k. Связь scatter-gather с широковещательным запросом MongoDB показывает, что ты видишь общий паттерн.

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

In Milvus, what are a growing segment, a sealed segment and the write-ahead log? Which design principles of Milvus do they implement, and why are fresh vectors not indexed with IVF or HNSW straight away?

Показать образец ответа

Every insert is first written by a proxy to the write-ahead log, Kafka or Pulsar — a to-do list for the data nodes. A growing segment is an in-memory segment that replays the shard's data from the log broker; it uses a FLAT index, that is brute force, so that fresh data is searchable at once and can still be appended. When it is full it is sealed: it becomes immutable, is flushed to object storage, and an index node builds an efficient index for it, such as IVF or HNSW. A search scans the growing segment, uses the indexes of the sealed segments, and merges the results. This implements "Separate Streaming and historical data" and "Log As data", and relies on "Disaggregate storage and computation", since nodes rebuild their state from the log and from object storage. Fresh vectors are not indexed straight away because such indexes are costly to build and hard to update: they are built once, on data that no longer changes.

Экзаменатор проверяет два вида сегментов с их индексами (FLAT против эффективного), роль лога как первой и основной копии и «почему»: индексу нужны данные, которые перестали меняться. Сравнение с commit log, memtable и SSTable в Cassandra — приятный бонус.

Урок 28 · Векторные базы данных: FAISS, Pinecone, Milvus — и экзаменационное «заполни пропуски» · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

In Milvus Lite you need every entity of demo_collection with a price above 50 in the category 'books', and you have no query vector. Which call fits?

  • client.get(collection_name="demo_collection", ids=[123, 456]) get выбирает по первичному ключу; а здесь у тебя условие, а не id.
  • client.search(collection_name="demo_collection", data=[query_vector], filter="price > 50 and category == 'books'", limit=5) search нужен вектор-запрос, и он возвращает только limit самых похожих совпадений, а не все.
  • client.query(collection_name="demo_collection", filter="price > 50 and category == 'books'") Скалярный запрос: без вектора, и он "Returns all entities that match the filter conditions" (p. 1706).
  • client.delete(collection_name="demo_collection", filter="price > 50 and category == 'books'") Тот же фильтр, но сущности удаляются, а не возвращаются.
Показать образец ответа

client.query with the filter: a scalar query needs no vector and returns all the entities that match the condition. client.search would need a query vector and rank the matches, client.get selects by id, and client.delete removes.

Search, query, get — три глагола со слайда (p. 1706). Ключевое различие — участвует ли вектор; "all entities that match" — это скалярный запрос.

Урок 29 · Feature stores

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 1 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Define training-serving skew as presented in the lecture. Explain why it arises in machine-learning systems, how a feature store prevents it, and how it differs from data drift.

Показать образец ответа

Training-serving skew, also called online/offline skew, is the situation in which a model performs poorly because the features used in production differ from the ones used during training. It arises because features are required both in the modelling phase and in the production (deployment) phase of the lifecycle: when each phase has its own feature code, the code is duplicated and the two implementations can silently diverge, for example in the time window, in the handling of refunds or in the default for missing values. A feature store prevents it by keeping one registered and versioned definition per feature and one transformation pipeline, whose results are stored in an offline store, which provides historical values for training, and in an online store, which provides the latest values with low latency for inference, both accessed through one serving API. In this way the same feature transformations are applied in training and in production. Data drift is different: the distribution of the input data changes over time while the computation stays the same; a feature store can detect drift by monitoring the features, but the remedy is retraining.

Полный ответ состоит из трёх частей: определение словами слайда (p. 734) вместе с синонимом (p. 743), причина — два пути кода для одного признака (p. 743) — и механизм: одно определение, два хранилища. Противопоставление дрейфу показывает понимание, а не память.

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 2 из 7 · открыть в уроке

In a feature store, which component typically stores only the latest feature values for each entity and is usually eventually consistent?

  • The offline store Оно держит "months' or years' worth of feature data for training purposes" (p. 765): историю, а не только последние значения.
  • The registry Реестр хранит определения и их метаданные (p. 769), а не значения признаков.
  • The online store "They typically only store the latest feature values for each entity" и "Online stores are usually eventually consistent" (p. 765).
  • The data source В Feast это сырые данные, которыми владеют пользователи; "Feast does not manage any of the raw underlying data" (p. 759).
Показать образец ответа

The online store. It persists the latest feature value of each entity for low-latency lookups during inference, usually in a key-value store such as Redis or DynamoDB, and it is usually eventually consistent. The offline store keeps months or years of history for training.

Каждый отвлекающий вариант — другой компонент той же архитектуры; два свойства из вопроса — именно те, что слайд (p. 765) приписывает онлайн-хранилищу.

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 3 из 7 · открыть в уроке

True or false: in Feast, for offline use cases that rely only on batch data, the data must first be ingested into the feature store.

  • True Слайд говорит обратное: "Feast does not need to ingest data and can query your existing data" (p. 757).
  • False Загрузка (ingestion) нужна для онлайн-сценариев: материализация из батчевых источников и push потоковых признаков (p. 757).
Показать образец ответа

False. For offline use cases that rely only on batch data, Feast does not need to ingest data and queries the existing data in place. Data are ingested only for online use cases: batch features are materialized into the online store, and streaming features are pushed to make them available both offline and online.

Вопрос «верно/неверно» легко построить, перевернув одну фразу слайда. С возможным штрафом за неверный ответ отвечай только то, что подтверждает фраза со слайда (p. 757).

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 4 из 7 · открыть в уроке

In Feast, what are an entity, a feature view and an entity key? Give an example of a feature view with zero entities, one with one entity and one with several entities, and explain why Feast uses a time-series data model.

Показать образец ответа

An entity is a collection of semantically related features, defined by the users to map to the domain of their use case, for example a driver or a customer. A feature view is a collection of features, bound to its entities and to a data source; it maps to zero or more entities. A collection of entities for a feature view is called an entity key; in the data, the entity key of a row is the tuple of its entity values, for example (customer_A, driver_1001). Zero entities: a global feature such as num_daily_global_transactions. One entity: a user feature such as user_age or last_5_bought_items. Several entities, a composite key: num_user_purchases_in_merchant_category, for a user and a merchant category. Feast uses a time-series data model, in which every row carries a timestamp, the entity values and the feature values, in order to build training datasets and to materialize features into an online store: the timestamps let a training row receive the latest feature values at or before its own time, without values from the future, and they tell materialization which value is the latest.

Определения — почти дословно со слайда (p. 758), примеры тоже; составной ключ — часть, которую легче всего забыть. Последнее предложение выходит за рамки слайдов (point-in-time correctness описана в документации Feast): оно показывает, почему слайд (p. 759) так настаивает на времени.

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 5 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Which of the following features can only be produced by an on-demand transformation? Select all that apply.

  • User country Данные в покое (at rest): батчевое преобразование (p. 767).
  • Number of views per listing in the past hour Считается непрерывно из потока (Kafka, Kinesis, PubSub): потоковое преобразование.
  • Whether the user is currently in a supported location Зависит от "data that is only available at the time of the prediction" — где пользователь сейчас (p. 767).
  • The similarity score between a listing and the search query Запрос существует только в момент, когда он приходит: "These features cannot be pre-computed" (p. 767).
  • Product category Свойство данных в покое: батчевое преобразование.
Показать образец ответа

The third and the fourth. On-demand transformations produce features from data that is only available at the time of the prediction, coming from the user-facing application, so they cannot be pre-computed. User country and product category come from batch transformations of data at rest; views per listing in the past hour come from a streaming transformation.

Примеры — со слайда (p. 767), так что вопрос может использовать их дословно. Проверка, которую надо применить: существует ли вход до того, как придёт запрос? Если да, признак можно посчитать заранее.

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 6 из 7 · открыть в уроке

Consider the ER scenario of the exam of 12 Sep 2025, tracking farmers and their sales at local markets: People (Personal ID, Name, Surname, Age, Address, State) Buy Products (Product ID, Name, Quantity, Expiration Date), with a Buy Date and an Amount; Products are sold at Markets (Name, Location, Opening Time, Closing Time, Size).

A cooperative wants two models: (a) every night, predict for each product the amount that will be bought the next day; (b) at the moment a person pays, decide whether the purchase is fraudulent. Using a feature store, propose for each model its entity key and features of the three transformation types where they fit, say which store serves each prediction, and which Feast retrieval method is used for training and for prediction.

Показать образец ответа

(a) Entity Product, entity key Product ID. Batch features computed from the history of Buy: the total Amount bought in the last 7 days, the number of days to the Expiration Date. Training: get_historical_features with an entity dataframe of (Product ID, date) rows labelled with the amount of the next day, so that each row is joined with the feature values at or before its date. The nightly prediction for all products is a batch prediction: it also uses get_historical_features on the offline store; no low latency is needed.

(b) Entity Person, entity key Personal ID. Batch feature: Age. Streaming feature: the number of purchases of this person in the last 10 minutes, computed from the stream of Buy events and pushed to both stores. On-demand feature: whether the market of the payment is open at the time of the payment, computed at request time from the request data and the market's Opening Time and Closing Time; it cannot be precomputed. Training: get_historical_features on past purchases labelled fraudulent or not. Prediction: get_online_features for the Personal ID, served by the online store with the latest values and low latency, plus the on-demand transformation at request time.

Наше упражнение, а не из прошлых экзаменов: сценарий взят, чтобы потренировать словарь в контексте. Ловушка — пункт (a): ночное предсказание — это офлайн, ровно как "Predicting user churn for all users on a daily basis" на слайде (p. 762), так что оно использует get_historical_features, а не онлайн-хранилище.

Урок 29 · Feature stores · вопрос 7 из 7 · открыть в уроке

According to the lecture, which of these feature stores are open-source? Select all that apply.

  • Feast "Type: Open-source" (p. 746).
  • Tecton "Type: Commercial" (p. 748).
  • Hopsworks "Type: Open-source (with enterprise version)" (p. 747).
  • AWS SageMaker Feature Store "Proprietary (part of AWS SageMaker)" (p. 749).
  • Featureform "Type: Open-source" (p. 754).
  • Databricks Feature Store "Proprietary (part of Databricks)" (p. 750).
Показать образец ответа

Feast, Hopsworks and Featureform. Tecton is commercial; AWS SageMaker, Databricks, Google Vertex AI and Azure ML feature stores are proprietary parts of their platforms, and Scribble Data Enrich is proprietary too.

Чистое запоминание слайдов (pp. 746–754): три open-source, одно коммерческое, пять проприетарных. Сомневаться стоит на Hopsworks — слайд записывает его как open-source с enterprise-версией.
